```
def auto_conv_max(a):
    n=len(a)
    if n <= 384:
        c=np.convolve(a,a)
    else:
        size=2*n-1
        m=1 << (size-1).bit_length()
        A=np.fft.rfft(a,m)
        c=np.fft.irfft(A*A,m)[:size]
    return float(c.max())
```
But repeated many, avoid allocate huge? okay.

```
def safe_eval(a):
    try:
       arr=np.asarray(a,dtype=float).ravel()
       arr=np.clip(arr,0,1000)
       if arr.size==0: return inf
       s=arr.sum()
       if s<0.01: return inf
       val=evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in arr])
       return float(val)
    except: return inf
```

LP alternating function:
```
def lp_optimize(p, time_limit, scaler, use_lls=True):
    p=norm(p)
    cur=scorer(p); best=p
    alpha=0.5
    no_improve=0
    while time:
       T = max_conv(p) # normalized
       q=lp_direction(p,T)
       if q is None: break
       p_new = line_search(p,q,scorer)
       if score(p_new)<cur-1e-10: accept
       else: maybe q direction with smaller alpha? If none, perturb p with random and restart? maybe reduce alpha etc.
```
For nonnegative objective actual, T lower bound 1/n; q uniform; no improvement if p not uniform? Wait if p not uniform, T>1/n; LP q likely not uniform and can reduce. It should converge to uniform. Good.

def softmax_normalize(y):
    y = y - np.max(y)
    e = np.exp(y)
    s = e.sum()
    if s==0 or not finite: return ones/n
    return e/s

def smooth_loss_grad(y, beta, use_fft=True):
    p = softmax_normalize(y)
    conv = auto_conv_full(p)
    # logsumexp
    maxc = conv.max()
    z = beta*conv - beta*maxc
    w = np.exp(z)
    ws = w.sum()
    if ws==0: w[argmax]=1
    else: w/=ws
    f = (maxc + np.log(ws))/beta
    # subgradient sg = sum over k w_k * p shifted (previous)
    sg = np.zeros(n)
    # for all k? Could use FFT: sg =? We can derive via convolution of w with p reversed.

Let's define scoring functions carefully.

`proxy_pct(a)`:
```
def proxy_score(a):
    arr=np.asarray(a,float)
    if arr.size==0: return inf
    arr=np.clip(arr,0,1000)
    s=arr.sum()
    if s < 0.01: return inf
    n=len(arr)
    b = auto_convolve_full(arr) # maybe direct/FFT
    mb=float(b.max())
    return float(2.0*n*mb/(s*s))
```
Since sum1 for internal, `2n*max`. For direct large, `np.convolve` direct maybe memory okay. Use threshold. For exact final, safe_eval.

`auto_convolve_full(a)`:
```
def auto_convolve_full(a):
   n=len(a)
   size=2*n-1
   if n <= 384:
      return np.convolve(a,a)
   m=1 << (size-1).bit_length()
   A=np.fft.rfft(a,m)
   return np.fft.irfft(A*A,m)[:size]
```
For n up to 5000, m=16384; okay. For local many n=256, direct use `np.convolve`. `np.convolve` for list? a ndarray. It returns full.

Implement `exact_score_fast(a)`:
```
def exact_score_fast(a):
   arr = np.asarray(a, dtype=float).ravel()
   arr = np.clip(arr,0,1000)
   s=arr.sum()
   if s<0.01: return inf
   n=arr.size
   if n <= 512:
      b=np.convolve(arr,arr)
   else:
      b=fft_convolve(arr)
   return float(2.0*n*b.max()/(s*s))
```

```
def line_search_p(p,q,cur_score,scorer):
    best=p,b=cur
    for alpha in (0.1,0.2,0.3,0.5,0.7,0.9,1.0,0.95,0.8): maybe 10 evals cheap.
       p_new = (1-alpha)*p + alpha*q
       p_new = normalize(p_new) # should sum1
       sc=scorer(p_new)
       if sc < b: b=sc
    return p_new at best, b
```
Since sum preserved if both sum1, no normalize needed but do to avoid.

Top peak sensitivity function:
```
def top_peak_sensitivity(conv,p,frac=0.1):
   n=len(p)
   maxc=conv.max()
   threshold=maxc*0.85
   idx=np.where(conv>=threshold)[0]
   if idx.size==0: idx=np.array([np.argmax(conv)])
   if idx.size>20:
       order=np.argsort(conv[idx])[::-1][:20]
       idx=idx[order]
   w=conv[idx]/conv[idx].sum()
   sens=np.zeros(n)
   for k,wk in zip(idx,w):
      js=max(0,k-n+1); je=min(n-1,k)
      # derivative factor: for j in js..je, l=k-j (0..n-1), add wk * p[l]
      # p slice k-je:k-js+1 reversed? For js..je: l=k-j. Values p[l]. We can assign:
      sens[js:je+1] += wk * p[k-je:k-js+1][::-1]
   return sens
```

def normalize_positive(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=float).ravel()
    a=np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a=np.maximum(a,0.0)
    s=a.sum()
    if s <= 1e-300: return None
    return a/s
```

10. `cross_corr_valid(w,p)`:
```
def cross_corr_valid(w,p):
    n=p.size
    if n <= 768:
       return np.correlate(w, p, mode='valid')
    # FFT
    size=1 << (3*n - 3 -1).bit_length() # need >=3n-3
    Wr=np.fft.rfft(w[::-1], size)
    P=np.fft.rfft(p,size)
    h=np.fft.irfft(P*Wr, size)
    m=2*n-1
    return h[m-1 : m-n-1 : -1]
```
Check length. If h length size, indices: m-1 down to m-n inclusive. Python slice `h[m-1 : m-n-1 : -1]` if m-n-1 can be negative? Example n=2,m=3,m-n-1=0. h[2:0:-1] -> indices 2 then? Stop exclusive, so h[2] only; need include h[1]. For stop m-n-1 =0, it stops before 0, includes 2,1? Python slice with negative step: start 2, stop 0, step -1: indices 2,1? It stops when index <= stop? It includes 1 because >0. Good length2. For n=3,m=5,m-n-1=1, h[4:1:-1] -> 4,3,2. Good. If m-n-1 negative for n? m=2n-1, m-n-1=n-2 >=0 for n>=2. For n=1, handle separately. Good.
Direct `np.correlate(w,p,'valid')`: Check with n=2, w len3 p len2, valid len2: [w0p0+w1p1, w1p0+w2p1]? Python earlier? We used p*wrev convention. Need direct check: np.correlate(w,p,'valid') result[0]=w0*p0 + w1*p1? result[1]=w1*p0 + w2*p1? That matches sg0? We wanted sg0=w0*p0+w1*p1, sg1=w1*p0+w2*p1. Yes. Good.

11. `top_peak_refine_weights(conv, top_frac=0.05, top_min=5, top_max=50)` returns normalized weights over lags.
```
def top_peak_weights(conv, n, frac=0.05):
    m=conv.size; maxc=conv.max()
    thresh=maxc*0.9
    idx=np.where(conv>=thresh)[0]
    if len(idx)==0: idx=np.array([np.argmax(conv)])
    max_count=max(5,min(80, int(n*0.1)))
    if len(idx)>max_count:
       order=np.argsort(conv[idx])[::-1][:max_count]
       idx=idx[order]
    w=np.zeros(m); w[idx]=conv[idx]; s=w.sum(); if s>0: w/=s else: w[np.argmax(conv)]=1
    return w
```
But top min maybe 10. For n small, count. Use `conc`? Good.

13. `lp_conv_matrix(p)`: top-level.
```
def lp_conv_matrix(p):
    n=p.size; m=2*n-1
    indptr=np.zeros(m+1,dtype=np.int64)
    # compute lengths and data
    lens=np.minimum(np.arange(m), n)  # row length? Actually for k=0 length1; k=n-1 length n; k=2n-2 length1.
    # formula: lens = np.minimum(np.minimum(k+1,m-k), n)
    k=np.arange(m)
    lens=np.minimum(np.minimum(k+1,m-k),n)
    indptr[1:]=np.cumsum(lens)
    total=int(indptr[-1])
    cols=np.empty(total,dtype=np.int64)
    data=np.empty(total,dtype=float)
    pos=0
    for k in range(m):
       js=int(max(0,k-n+1)); je=int(min(n-1,k)); ln=je-js+1
       columns slice js..je. We can fill with np.arange? To avoid frequent arange, use a precomputed? For n large, could do:
       cols[pos:pos+ln] = js + np.arange(ln) # creates array.
       data[pos:pos+ln] = p[k-je:k-js+1][::-1]
       pos += ln
    return sp.csr_matrix((data, cols, indptr), shape=(m,n))
```

14. `lp_direction(p, solve_time=5.0)`:
```
def lp_direction(p, time_limit=10.0):
   if not SCIPY_OPT_AVAILABLE or sp is None: return None
   n=p.size
   if n>1200: # too heavy? Maybe 1536? Use 1024 max for many, but allow 2048 one? Set max 1536.
       return None
   A=lp_conv_matrix(p)
   T? We scale constraints to 1; A values p. b=ones(2n-1)
   c=-np.ones(n)
   bounds=[(0,None)]*n
   try:
       res=linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=np.ones(A.shape[0]), bounds=bounds, method='highs', options={'time_limit': max(0.5, time_limit), 'disp': False})
   except Exception:
       return None
   if res is None or not res.success: return None
   y=np.asarray(res.x)
   y=np.maximum(y,0)
   s=y.sum()
   if s <= 1e-12: return None
   return y/s
```
Note constraints A y <=1. A values p, sum p=1. This is finite. For p one-hot, optimum y entries up to1, sum n. Good.

15. `line_search_p(p,q,scorer,cur)`:
```
def line_search_p(p,q,scorer,cur):
   best=p; best_s=cur
   # alphas
   alphas=(0.05,0.1,0.2,0.3,0.5,0.7,0.9,1.0)
   for a in alphas:
      v=(1-a)*p + a*q
      s=v.sum()
      if s<=1e-300: continue
      v=v/s
      sc=scorer(v)
      if sc < best_s - 1e-12:
         best_s=sc; best=v.copy()
   return best,best_s
```
For scorer actual, line search calls actual many; avoid in actual local. For fast okay.

16. `lp_optimize(p,budget,scorer, max_iters=20, lp_time=5)`:
```
def lp_optimize(p0,budget,scorer,max_iters=20,lp_time=8):
   p=norm_positive(p0); if None return p0,inf
   cur=scorer(p); best=p; best_s=cur
   start=time.monotonic()
   stuck=0
   for it in range(max_iters):
      if time.monotonic() >= start+budget: break
      qlimit=min(lp_time, (start+budget-time.monotonic())*0.5)
      if qlimit <2: break
      q=lp_direction(p,qlimit)
      if q is None: break
      p_new,snew=line_search_p(p,q,scorer,cur)
      if snew < cur - 1e-10:
          p=p_new; cur=snew; best=p.copy(); best_s=cur; stuck=0
      else:
          # try q alone
          sq=scorer(q)
          if sq < cur -1e-10:
             p=q.copy(); cur=sq; best=p.copy(); best_s=cur
          else:
             stuck +=1
             if stuck>=3: break
             # perturb p and q? maybe p = mix with uniform small then q
   return best,best_s
```
Scorer likely fast. If scorer actual, too expensive; use fast for lp.

17. `lbfgs_smooth(p0,budget, use_time...)`:
Define smooth_pair top-level.
```
def smooth_pair(y, beta):
    p=softmax_normalize(y)
    n=p.size
    conv=auto_convolve(p)
    maxc=float(np.max(conv))
    z=beta*conv - beta*maxc
    # if beta*max huge? beta up to 10000, conv max 1/n, okay
    # But z for very small exp underflows; fine
    e=np.exp(z)
    s=e.sum()
    if not finite or s<=0: w=zeros; w[argmax]=1
    else w=e/s
    loss=(maxc + np.log(s))/beta
    # objective J=2n loss
    J=2.0*n*loss
    # derivative: sg = cross_corr_valid(w,p)
    sg=cross_corr_valid(w,p)
    gp=4.0*n*sg  # 2n * 2 sg
    gy=gp - p*(gp @ p)
    # If nonfinite, fallback
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(gy)): gy=np.zeros_like(p)
    return J, gy
```
But if n=0? no. `np.log(s)` when s maybe 0? handled.
`softmax_normalize`:
```
def softmax_normalize(y):
    y=np.asarray(y,float)
    y=y-np.max(y)
    e=np.exp(y)
    s=e.sum()
    if not finite or s<=0: return np.ones(len(y))/len(y)
    p=e/s
    # avoid zeros? softmax already >0 maybe underflow, but okay. If any zero, add tiny?
    return p
```
For L-BFGS, if underflow zeros, log later may be -inf. We can clip y after iterations? In `lbfgs`, after res, p=softmax; if min p <1e-300? softmax with y large differences can underflow to 0; but L-BFGS may push. We can renormalize by adding small: `p=np.maximum(p, 1e-300); p/=p.sum()`. Good.
`log_from_p(p)` uses `np.log(np.maximum(p,1e-300))`.

`lbfgs_smooth`:
```
def lbfgs_smooth(p0,budget,beta_list=(20,100,500,2500,10000,20000), max_iter_total=800):
   if not SCIPY_OPT_AVAILABLE: return p0,fast_score_norm(p0)
   p=norm_positive(p0); cur=fast_score_norm(p); best=p
   y=log_from_p(p)
   start=monotonic
   iters_left=max_iter_total
   for beta in beta_list:
     if time done or iters_left<=0: break
     # set maxiter proportional beta? maybe 200
     maxit=min(250, iters_left)
     options={'maxiter':maxit,'maxfun':maxit*2,'gtol':1e-8,'ftol':1e-12,'maxls':20}
     try:
       res=minimize(smooth_pair, y, jac=True, args=(beta,), method='L-BFGS-B', options=options)
     except Exception:
       break
     if res is None or res.x is None: break
     y=np.asarray(res.x,float)
     p=softmax_normalize(y)
     p=np.maximum(p,0); s=p.sum(); if s<=0: break; p/=s
     sc=fast_score_norm(p)
     if sc < cur -1e-10:
        cur=sc; best=p.copy(); iters_left=0? no just continue
     # reset y = log(p)
     y=log_from_p(p)
     iters_left -= maxit
     # if sc finite record
   # final maybe short local? return best, sc
```
But L-BFGS `minimize` with `args=(beta,)`: It will call `smooth_pair(x, *args)`; with `jac=True`, returns list? It expects fun(x, *args) returns (f,g). Good.

18. `subg_optimize(p,budget,scorer, beta_list)` maybe optional. Since L-BFGS covers, maybe skip to save time. But L-BFGS uses fast metric; if fast not actual, maybe not. Could implement simple top-gradient descent quickly.
```
def subgradient_optimize(p0,budget,scorer,beta_list=(10,50,500),lr_init=0.03):
   p=norm; cur=scor(p); best
   for beta in beta_list:
      lr=lr_init
      stuck=0
      while time:
         conv=auto_convolve(p)
         w=top_peak_weights(conv) or softmax with beta? Use top_peak maybe group max.
         sg=cross_corr_valid(w,p)
         # Descent: p_new = project(p - lr*sg)
         v=p - lr*sg
         v=project_simplex(v)
         # line search mix α
         p_new,snew=line_search_p(p,v,scorer,cur)
         if snew<cur-1e-10: accept; stuck=0
         else: lr*=0.6; stuck++ if stuck>5 break or change beta
   return best
```
This is similar PGD but with top weights and line search. Use fast scorer.

22. `fast_local_refine(p,budget,rng,scorer)`: as above. For speed, we can combine actual or fast scorer. It will use `auto_convolve` each accepted move. For n=256, direct. For n large, FFT.
Detailed implementation:
```
def local_refine(p0, budget, rng, scorer, refresh_every=1, T0=0.05, Tfloor=0.001, use_peak=True):
    p=normalize_positive(p0)
    if p is None: return p0, inf
    cur=float(scorer(p))
    best=p.copy(); best_s=cur
    if not np.isfinite(cur):
       cur=fast_score_norm(p); best_s=cur
    start=time.monotonic()
    conv=auto_convolve(p)
    no_improve=0
    it=0
    while time.monotonic() < start+budget:
        frac=min(1.0,(time.monotonic()-start)/budget)
        T=T0*(1-frac)**2 + Tfloor
        # move type
        r=rng.random()
        if use_peak and r<0.7:
           sens=top_peak_sensitivity(conv,p)
           maxsens=float(sens.max())
           if maxsens <=1e-15:
              # random transfer
           else:
              src_w=np.maximum(sens,0.0)*(p+1e-12)+1e-15
              src_w/=src_w.sum()
              tgt_w=np.maximum(1.0, maxsens - sens)+1e-6  # low sens
              # maybe weight by uniform, not by p
              tgt_w/=tgt_w.sum()
              i=np.random.choice? Use rng.choice with p.
              j=rng.choice( n, p=tgt_w)
              # ensure i!=j; if same retry
              delta=p[i]*rng.uniform(0.002,0.04)
        elif r<0.85:
           # random transfer
           i=rng.integers(n); j=rng.integers(n); delta=p[i]*rng.uniform(0.001,0.03)
        else:
           # perturb whole: log noise sigma decreasing
           sigma=0.05*(1-frac)+0.005
           lp=np.log(p+1e-300)+rng.normal(0,sigma,n)
           p_new=softmax_normalize(lp)
           sc=scorer(p_new)
           accept SA
           continue
        # perform move
        if i==j: continue
        if delta>p[i]: delta=p[i]*0.5
        old_i=p[i]; old_j=p[j]
        p[i]-=delta; p[j]+=delta
        sc=float(scorer(p))
        if sc < cur + 1e-12:
           cur=sc
           if sc < best_s:
               best_s=sc; best=p.copy()
           conv=auto_convolve(p)  # refresh
           no_improve=0
        else:
           if rng.random() < math.exp(-(sc-cur)/max(T,1e-9)):
               cur=sc
               conv=auto_convolve(p)
               # don't update best if worse
           else:
               p[i]=old_i; p[j]=old_j
               no_improve +=1
        it+=1
        # periodic operations
        if it % 50 ==0:
           # try symmetrize and smoothing with score
           for cand in [symmetrize(p), smoothed(p,2)]: if scorer(cand)<cur: accept...
    return best,best_s
```
Need use `rng.choice` for p arrays; for large n, building src_w each move. For n up to 1024, okay. For n larger, maybe less.

That's C-level O(n^2) but optimized. `np.correlate` direct for n=256 65k per move, 50k ->3B maybe high. But many moves? Maybe time. Could use only top peaks sparse assignments maybe lower if few peaks. But top peaks maybe many. We can reduce: select top 5-10 lags, assign slices; each up to n length =10*256=2560 operations in Python loop? With numpy slice assignments, maybe okay. Use top_max=8. Good. Let's implement `top_peak_sensitivity_sparse`:
```
def top_peak_sensitivity_sparse(conv,p,max_top=8,frac=0.1):
   n=p.size; maxc=conv.max(); thresh=max(0,0.95*maxc)
   idx=np.where(conv>=thresh)[0]
   if idx.size==0: idx=np.array([np.argmax(conv)])
   if idx.size>max_top:
      top_indices = idx[np.argsort(conv[idx])[::-1][:max_top]]
   else: top_indices=idx
   w = conv[top_indices]; s=w.sum(); if s<=0: w=np.ones(len)/len else w/=s
   sens=np.zeros(n)
   for k,wk in zip(top_indices,w):
       js=max(0,k-n+1); je=min(n-1,k)
       if js<=je:
          sens[js:je+1] += wk * p[k-je:k-js+1][::-1]
   return sens
```
With max_top=8, at most 8 loops; slice assignment uses C copy of slice of p (view) multiplied. Good. Use max_top adapt: for n small 4, for n large 12? Set `max_top = min(12, max(4, n//64))`.
Then source/target selection efficient.

Refresh `conv` after accepted move. If actual scorer but conv proxy, need refresh for sens. Use `auto_convolve`. For n=256 direct 65k per accepted. If many accepted maybe okay. If almost all accepted early, many; but as improve slows, accepted less. Use SA acceptance; if T high, accept many worse -> conv refresh each accepted; could be costly. Limit T and only refresh every few accepted? Sens may stale. Use refresh_on_accept always for simplicity. If too slow, can toggle.

For actual local, scor hints expensive; but conv fast. Good.

def add_unique_sequence(seq_list, p):
   p=normalize_positive(p)
   if p is None: return
   for q in seq_list:
      if q.size==p.size and float(np.dot(q,p)) > 0.999: return
   seq_list.append(p)
```

`project_simplex` from previous:
```
def project_simplex(v):
    n=v.size
    if n==0: return v.copy()
    u=np.sort(v)[::-1]
    cssv=np.cumsum(u)
    rho=np.nonzero(u*np.arange(1,n+1) - (cssv-1) >0)[0]
    if rho.size==0: return np.full(n,1/n)
    r=rho[-1]
    theta=(cssv[r]-1)/(r+1)
    return np.maximum(v-theta,0)
```
This projects to sum1? It assumes target sum1. Yes. If v sum not 1, works for 1. It returns nonnegative sum1. Good.

def fast_score_generic(a):
   arr=np.asarray(a,float).ravel()
   arr=np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
   arr=np.clip(arr,0,1000)
   s=arr.sum()
   if s<0.01: return inf
   n=arr.size
   b=auto_convolve(arr/s? Or use ratio with original sum: max conv scales with s^2, objective =2n maxconv(a)/s^2. For large s, overflow? Use normalized p=arr/s; conv_p=auto_convolve(p); maxconv(a)=s^2 maxconv(p). score=2n*maxconv(p). So normalize.
   p=arr/s
   maxc=auto_convolve(p).max()
   return 2n*maxc
```

Candidate generation details function:
```
def generate_seed_candidates(parent, lengths, rng):
    cands=[]
    for n in lengths:
       if n < 4: continue
       if parent is not None:
           p=interp_to_length(parent,n)
           if p is not None: add_unique_sequence(cands,p)
       # uniform
       add_unique_sequence(cands, np.ones(n)/n)
       # gaussian
       if n>8:
          x=np.arange(n,float)
          for sf in (0.15,0.3):
             g=np.exp(-0.5*((x-n*0.5)/(sf*n))**2)
             add...
       # random dirichlet
       for alpha in (0.3,0.8,2.0): maybe too many.
          add_unique_sequence(cands, rng.dirichlet(np.ones(n)*alpha))
       # bimodal
       w=max(1,n//6); bi=zeros; bi[:w]=1; bi[-w:]=1; norm; add
       # symmetric noise around uniform
       z=norm; z += rng.normal(0,0.2,z.size); z=max; normalize; add
       # chirp
       if n>16: c=0.5*(1+cos(2π x*x/(2n))); norm; add
    return cands
```

    p=normalize_positive(p0)
    if p is None: return None, inf
    n=p.size
    best=normalize_positive(p0)

    cur_fast=fast_score_norm(p)
    start=time.monotonic(); end=start+budget
    # quick local
    b1 = min(60.0, (end-time.monotonic())*0.25)

       scorer = safe_eval if (use_actual_local_global and n<=900) else fast_score_generic
       p1,b=local_refine(p,b1,rng,scorer)
       p2=normalize_positive(p1)

    # fast gradient if close_fast

    return best,best_sc

`mutate_sequence` top-level:
```
def mutate_sequence(p, sigma, rng, moves=3):
   p=normalize_positive(p)
   y=np.log(np.maximum(p,1e-300))
   y += rng.normal(0.0, sigma, p.size)
   q=softmax_normalize(y)
   q=np.maximum(q,0); s=q.sum(); if s<=0: return p.copy(); q/=s
   for _ in range(moves):
       i=int(rng.integers(q.size)); j=int(rng.integers(q.size))
       if i==j: continue
       d=q[i]*float(rng.uniform(0.001,0.03))
       q[i]-=d; q[j]+=d
       q=np.maximum(q,0); s=q.sum(); if s>0: q/=s
   return q
```
`smoothed_p(p,width)` and `symmetrize_p(p)` top-level.

Let's implement function details precisely.

`normalize_positive` handles lists/arrays. If sum is nonfinite, replace. Use `np.isfinite`.
```
def normalize_positive(a):
    arr = np.asarray(a, dtype=float).ravel()
    if arr.size == 0: return None
    arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
    arr = np.maximum(arr, 0.0)
    s = float(arr.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return None
    return arr / s
```

`get_parent_sequence`:
```
def get_parent_sequence():
    try:
        p = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=float).ravel()
    except Exception:
        return None
    p = np.where(np.isfinite(p),np.maximum(p,0.0),0.0)
    s=p.sum()
    if p.size>0 and np.isfinite(s) and s>1e-100:
        # maybe not normalize? return original? If return original for interp, normalization later; but if original values scale huge, interp okay. Return as is clipped?
        return p
    return None
```
Maybe clip 1000? Parent may >1000; eval clips. But normalize positive will clip? normalize_negative does not clip 1000. It clips negatives and normalizes, but if values >1000 after normalization? `interp_to_length`:
```
def interp_to_length(a,n):
    a=normalize_positive(a)
    if a is None: return None
    if a.size == n: return a.copy()
    x_old=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,a.size)
    x_new=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
    out=np.interp(x_new,x_old,a)
    return normalize_positive(out)
```

`add_unique_sequence`:
```
def add_unique_sequence(seq_list,p):
    p=normalize_positive(p)
    if p is None: return
    for q in seq_list:
        if q.size == p.size:
            denom=max(float(np.dot(q,p)),1e-300)
            if denom > 0.9995: return
    seq_list.append(p)
```
For different sizes, not unique. If parent length 256 and uniform length 256, dot maybe not 1 if parent nonuniform; unique.

`auto_convolve`:
```
def auto_convolve(a):
    n = len(a)
    if n <= 0: return np.array([0.0])
    if n <= 384:
        return np.convolve(a.astype(float, copy=False), a.astype(float, copy=False))
    size=2*n-1
    m=1 << (size-1).bit_length()
    A=np.fft.rfft(a, m)
    c=np.fft.irfft(A*A, m)
    return c[:size]
```
Use `a.astype(float,copy=False)`. For direct, if a integer? arrays float. Good.

`fast_score_generic`:
```
def fast_score_generic(a):
    p=normalize_positive(a)
    if p is None: return float('inf')
    n=p.size
    c=auto_convolve(p)
    mb=float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(mb) or mb < 0.0: mb=0.0
    return float(2.0*n*mb)
```
Since p sum1.

`safe_eval`:
```
_ORACLE_AVAILABLE = True
def safe_eval(a):
    global _ORACLE_AVAILABLE
    try:
        arr=np.asarray(a,dtype=float).ravel()
        if arr.size==0: return float('inf')
        arr=np.where(np.isfinite(arr),arr,0.0)
        arr=np.maximum(arr,0.0)
        arr=np.minimum(arr,1000.0)
        if arr.size==0: return float('inf')
        s=arr.sum()
        if s < 0.01:
            # try scale up
            s2=float(arr.sum())
            if s2 <=0: return inf
            arr = arr * (1.0/s2)
        # evaluate expects list
        val=evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in arr])
        val=float(val)
        return val if np.isfinite(val) else float('inf')
    except NameError:
        _ORACLE_AVAILABLE=False
        return float('inf')
    except Exception:
        return float('inf')
```

`softmax_normalize`:
```
def softmax_normalize(y):
    y=np.asarray(y,dtype=float).ravel()
    if y.size==0: return y.copy()
    ym=y-np.max(y)
    e=np.exp(ym)
    s=e.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s<=1e-300:
        return np.ones(y.size)/y.size
    p=e/s
    p=np.maximum(p,0.0)
    s2=p.sum()
    if s2<=1e-300: return ones
    return p/s2
```

`log_from_p`:
```
def log_from_p(p):
    p=np.asarray(p,float)
    return np.log(np.maximum(p,1e-300))
```

`cross_corr_valid`:
```
def cross_corr_valid(w,p):
    n=p.size
    m=2*n-1
    if n <= 768:
        return np.correlate(w, p, mode='valid')
    needed=m+n-1
    msize=1 << (needed-1).bit_length()
    Wr=np.fft.rfft(w.astype(float, copy=False)[::-1], msize)
    P=np.fft.rfft(p.astype(float, copy=False), msize)
    h=np.fft.irfft(P*Wr, msize)
    # h[m-1 : m-n-1 : -1]
    return h[m-1 : m-n-1 : -1]
```
For n=1: `needed=1+1-1=1`, msize=1, h length1, slice h[0: -? m-n-1 = -1] -> `h[0:-1:-1]`? For start0 stop -1 step -1 gives empty? Need handle n=1 separately. Let's add if n==1: return p.copy()*w[0]? sg0 = w0*p0. `np.correlate` direct for n=1 works: w len1, p len1, valid len1. Our direct branch n<=768 handles n=1. Good. For n>768, m-n-1 positive.

`smooth_pair`:
```
def smooth_pair(y,beta):
    p=softmax_normalize(y)
    if p is None: return (1e100, np.zeros(...)) but p not None
    n=p.size
    conv=auto_convolve(p)
    maxc=float(np.max(conv))
    if not np.isfinite(beta) or beta <=0: beta=1.0
    z=beta*conv - beta*maxc
    # clip z >= -50 to avoid underflow? exp(-100) ~3e-44 okay but sum still okay. For large beta, many underflow to0. Use np.exp(np.maximum(z, -745))? But z <=0, no overflow; underflow okay. However if z has -inf due beta*conv inf? conv finite. Use z=np.where(np.isfinite(z), z, -745)
    e=np.exp(z)
    s=e.sum()
    if not finite or s<=0:
       w=np.zeros_like(conv); w[int(np.argmax(conv))]=1.0
    else: w=e/s
    loss=(maxc + math.log(float(s)))/float(beta)
    if not np.isfinite(loss): loss=1e100
    sg=cross_corr_valid(w,p)
    gp=4.0*float(n)*sg
    gsum=float(np.dot(gp,p))
    gy=gp - p*gsum
    gy=np.where(np.isfinite(gy), gy, 0.0)
    J=2.0*float(n)*loss
    if not np.isfinite(J): J=1e100
    return float(J), gy
```
Need import math. Or use `np.log`. Use math.

`lbfgs_smooth`:
```
def lbfgs_smooth(p0,budget):
    if not SCIPY_OPT_AVAILABLE:
       return normalize_positive(p0), fast_score_generic(p0)
    p=normalize_positive(p0)
    if p is None: return None, inf
    cur=fast_score_normalized(p)
    best=p.copy()
    y=log_from_p(p)
    start=time.monotonic()
    betas=(20.0, 100.0, 500.0, 2500.0, 10000.0)
    total_iter=0
    for beta in betas:
       elapsed=time.monotonic()-start
       if elapsed>=budget: break
       rem=budget-elapsed
       maxit=min(200, max(20, int(rem*2.0)))  # heuristic
       options={'maxiter':maxit,'maxfun':maxit*2,'ftol':1e-12,'gtol':1e-8,'maxls':20}
       try:
          res=minimize(smooth_pair, y, jac=True, args=(beta,), method='L-BFGS-B', options=options)
       except Exception:
          break
       if res is None or res.x is None: break
       y=np.asarray(res.x,dtype=float).ravel()
       if not np.all(np.isfinite(y)): break
       p=softmax_normalize(y)
       p=np.maximum(p,0); s=p.sum(); if s<=0: break; p=p/s
       sc=fast_score_normalized(p)
       if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < cur - 1e-10:
           cur=sc; best=p.copy(); y=log_from_p(p)
       # even if not improve, reset y to current best? To avoid drift, use best.
       # Actually L-BFGS may worsen; if so, continue from best not res to ensure progress.
       if not (np.isfinite(sc) and sc < cur + 1e-8):
           y=log_from_p(best)
       else:
           y=log_from_p(p)
       total_iter += maxit
       if cur <= 2.0 + 1e-8 and n? maybe can break? For standard lower bound 2, break if near 2.
       if cur <= 1.505 + 1e-8? no target.
    return best, cur
```
Potential issue: If n standard, lower bound 2; L-BFGS will converge to uniform at 2. If cur near 2, we can break to save. But if hidden target 1.5, fast lower bound 2, but `fast_score_normalized` cannot below2; if `close_fast` false, we won't call lbfgs. If close true, break at 2+1e-6. Good.
But for hidden fast? no.
Maybe don't hard break at 2 because if n=1? 2. If hidden allowed lower but fast not, we skip. Fine.
Add `if cur <= 2.0 + 1e-8 and n > 1: break`.
But if actual close false and we do call? no.

`lp_conv_matrix` as above. Need handle `sp` None.
```
def lp_conv_matrix(p):
    if sp is None: return None
    n=p.size; m=2*n-1
    indptr=np.zeros(m+1,dtype=np.int64)
    lens=np.minimum(np.minimum(np.arange(m,dtype=np.int64)+1, m - np.arange(m,dtype=np.int64)), n)
    indptr[1:]=np.cumsum(lens, dtype=np.int64)
    total=int(indptr[-1])
    cols=np.empty(total,dtype=np.int64)
    data=np.empty(total,dtype=float)
    pos=0
    for k in range(m):
       js = 0 if k < n-1 else k-n+1
       je = k if k < n else n-1
       ln=je-js+1
       cols[pos:pos+ln] = js + np.arange(ln, dtype=np.int64)
       data[pos:pos+ln] = p[k-je:k-js+1][::-1]
       pos += ln
    return sp.csr_matrix((data, cols, indptr), shape=(m,n))
```
For k < n-1: js=0, je=k; else k-n+1. For je: if k<n, je=k else n-1. Good. For n=1,m=1,k=0: js0,je0,ln1, p[0:1] reversed.
`lens` formula: m-k? `np.arange(m)` int; m - arange. `minimum(k+1, m-k)` okay. For k=m-1: m-k=1. Good.

`lp_direction`:
```
def lp_direction(p,time_limit):
    if not SCIPY_OPT_AVAILABLE or sp is None: return None
    n=p.size
    if n > 1200: return None
    A=lp_conv_matrix(p)
    if A is None: return None
    maxp=float(np.max(p)) if n else 1.0
    ub=max(100.0, 20.0/maxp if maxp>0 else 1e6)
    b=np.ones(A.shape[0], dtype=float)
    c=-np.ones(n,dtype=float)
    bounds=[(0.0, ub)]*n
    opts={'time_limit': max(1.0, float(time_limit)), 'disp': False}
    try:
        res=linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, bounds=bounds, method='highs', options=opts)
    except Exception:
        # retry without time limit? Maybe if option unsupported
        try:
            res=linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, bounds=bounds, method='highs', options={'disp':False})
        except Exception:
            return None
    if res is None or not res.success:
        return None
    y=np.asarray(res.x,dtype=float).ravel()
    y=np.where(np.isfinite(y), np.maximum(y,0.0), 0.0)
    s=float(y.sum())
    if s <= 1e-12: return None
    return y/s
```
`bounds=[(0.0, ub)]*n` creates list of same tuple references, okay. If ub huge maybe. Option time_limit might be unsupported in older HiGHS? It should. Fallback.

`line_search_p`:
```
def line_search_p(p,q,scorer,cur):
    best=p.astype(float,copy=True) if p is not None else p
    best_s=cur
    alphas=(0.05,0.1,0.2,0.3,0.5,0.7,0.9,1.0)
    for a in alphas:
       v=(1.0-a)*p + a*q
       v=normalize_positive(v)
       if v is None: continue
       sc=float(scorer(v))
       if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_s - 1e-12:
           best_s=sc; best=v.copy()
    return best,best_s
```
If `cur` inf, best_s inf; first finite accepted. Good.

`lp_optimize`:
```
def lp_optimize(p0,budget,scorer):
    p=normalize_positive(p0)
    if p is None: return None, inf
    cur=float(scorer(p))
    best=p.copy(); best_s=cur
    start=time.monotonic()
    stuck=0
    lp_time=max(1.0, min(15.0, budget/4.0))
    while time.monotonic()-start < budget:
       rem=budget-(time.monotonic()-start)
       if rem < 3: break
       if not SCIPY_OPT_AVAILABLE: break
       if p.size > 1200: break
       q=lp_direction(p, min(lp_time, rem*0.6))
       if q is None: break
       p_new,snew=line_search_p(p,q,scorer,cur)
       if snew < cur - 1e-10:
           p=p_new; cur=snew; best=p_new.copy(); best_s=cur; stuck=0
       else:
           qn=q
           sq=float(scorer(qn))
           if np.isfinite(sq) and sq < cur - 1e-10:
              p=qn.copy(); cur=sq; best=p.copy(); best_s=cur; stuck=0
           else:
              stuck +=1
              if stuck >= 3: break
              # perturb? p unchanged
    return best,best_s
```
If `scorer` is fast, good. If actual, too slow but okay if used. `line_search_p` uses scorer actual if actual; but we will pass fast.

`top_peak_sensitivity_sparse`:
```
def top_peak_sensitivity_sparse(conv,p):
    n=p.size
    if conv.size==0: return np.zeros(n)
    maxc=float(np.max(conv))
    if not np.isfinite(maxc) or maxc <=0: 
       # fallback uniform
       return np.full(n, 1.0/(n if n else 1))
    thresh=0.95*maxc
    idx=np.where(conv >= thresh)[0]
    if idx.size == 0:
       idx=np.array([int(np.argmax(conv))])
    max_top = int(min(12, max(4, n//64)))
    if idx.size > max_top:
       order=np.argsort(conv[idx])[::-1][:max_top]
       idx=idx[order]
    wv=conv[idx].astype(float)
    ws=float(wv.sum())
    if ws <= 0 or not np.isfinite(ws):
       wv=np.ones_like(wv)/wv.size
    else:
       wv=wv/ws
    sens=np.zeros(n,dtype=float)
    for k,wk in zip(idx,wv):
       k=int(k); wk=float(wk)
       js=0 if k < n-1 else k-n+1
       je=k if k < n else n-1
       if js <= je:
          sens[js:je+1] += wk * p[k-je:k-js+1][::-1]
    return sens
```
Good.

`smoothed_p`:
```
def smoothed_p(p,width):
    p=normalize_positive(p)
    if p is None: return None
    n=p.size
    if n<3: return p.copy()
    # width parameter e.g. 1.0 = std in bins = 1? We'll call with int scales.
    radius=max(1, int(width*2))
    # Ensure radius not > n//2
    radius=min(radius, max(1,n//2))
    x=np.arange(-radius, radius+1, dtype=float)
    k=np.exp(-0.5*(x/float(max(1,int(width))))**2)
    k/=k.sum()
    out=np.convolve(p,k,mode='same')
    out=np.maximum(out,0)
    s=out.sum()
    if s<=0: return p.copy()
    return out/s
```
But `width` parameter ambiguous. Use `sigma` in bins. For sequence n, sigma = max(1, int(n*frac)). We'll call with e.g. scaled by n: `smoothed_p(p, max(1,int(n*0.01)))` etc. Define `width` as sigma.
```
def smoothed_p(p, sigma):
   if sigma <=0: return p.copy()
```
Good.

`symmetrize_p`:
```
def symmetrize_p(p):
   p=normalize_positive(p)
   if p is None: return None
   out=0.5*(p + p[::-1])
   s=out.sum()
   return out/s if s>0 else p.copy()
```

`mix_uniform`:
```
def mix_uniform(p, frac):
   p=normalize_positive(p)
   if p is None: return None
   n=p.size
   out=(1-frac)*p + frac*(1.0/n)
   s=out.sum(); return out/s
```

`mutate_sequence`:
```
def mutate_sequence(p,sigma,rng,moves=3):
   p=normalize_positive(p)
   if p is None: return None
   n=p.size
   y=log_from_p(p)
   if sigma>0:
      y += rng.normal(0.0,float(sigma),n)
   q=softmax_normalize(y)
   for _ in range(max(0,int(moves))):
      i=int(rng.integers(n)); j=int(rng.integers(n))
      if i==j: continue
      d=float(q[i])*float(rng.uniform(0.001,0.03))
      q[i]-=d; q[j]+=d
   q=np.maximum(q,0)
   s=q.sum()
   if s<=0: return p.copy()
   return q/s
```

`local_refine`: Need ensure `scorer` is top-level callable. It receives array. We'll use `float(scorer(v))`.
Potential performance: `rng.choice` with probability arrays for each peak move can be expensive if arrays size n. For n up to 1024, okay. In Python, `rng.choice(n,p=src_dist)` overhead O(n) to validate. We can instead use inverse CDF with `rng.random()`? Need cumulative sum each move O(n). `rng.choice` does similar. Fine.
But we build src_dist and target_dist each move. Could optimize by caching and updating after conv refresh. Simpler.
For `rng.choice(n, p=...)`, ensure p sums to1 and nonnegative, finite. Use `np.maximum(src_w,0)`.
Need choose target not same as source; if same, retry few times.

Let's write local_refine carefully:
```
def local_refine(p0, budget, rng, scorer):
    p=normalize_positive(p0)
    if p is None: return None, inf
    n=p.size
    cur=float(scorer(p))
    if not np.isfinite(cur):
        cur=fast_score_normalized(p)
    best=p.copy(); best_s=cur
    if not np.isfinite(best_s): best_s=1e100
    start=time.monotonic()
    conv=auto_convolve(p)
    it=0
    T0=0.04; Tfloor=0.0008
    while time.monotonic() - start < budget:
        frac=(time.monotonic()-start)/budget
        if frac > 1.0: frac=1.0
        T=T0*(1.0-frac)**2 + Tfloor
        r=float(rng.random())
        move_done=False
        if r < 0.65:
            sens=top_peak_sensitivity_sparse(conv,p)
            maxsens=float(np.max(sens))
            if np.isfinite(maxsens) and maxsens > 1e-15:
                src_w=np.maximum(sens,0.0)*(p+1e-12)+1e-15
                ss=src_w.sum()
                if ss>0 and np.isfinite(ss): src_w /= ss
                else: src_w=np.full(n,1.0/n)
                tgt_w=max(1e-12, maxsens - sens) + 1e-6
                ts=tgt_w.sum()
                if ts>0 and np.isfinite(ts): tgt_w /= ts
                else: tgt_w=np.full(n,1.0/n)
                i=int(rng.choice(n, replace=1, p=src_w)[0])
                # target choices avoid source low? choose target low sens but maybe nonzero
                j=int(rng.choice(n, replace=1, p=tgt_w)[0])
                if i == j:
                    # choose j as argmin sens or random
                    j=int(np.argmin(sens)) if n>1 else i
                d=float(p[i])*float(rng.uniform(0.002,0.04))
                move_done=True
            else:
                # fallback random
                pass
        if not move_done:
            # random or log noise
            r2=float(rng.random())
            if r2 < 0.7:
                i=int(rng.integers(n)); j=int(rng.integers(n))
                if i==j: continue
                d=float(p[i])*float(rng.uniform(0.001,0.03))
            else:
                sigma=0.05*(1.0-frac)+0.004
                q=mutate_sequence(p,sigma,rng,moves=0)
                if q is None: continue
                sc=float(scorer(q))
                if np.isfinite(sc):
                   if sc < cur or (rng.random()<math.exp(-(sc-cur)/max(T,1e-12))):
                       p=q; cur=sc
                       if sc < best_s: best_s=sc; best=p.copy()
                       conv=auto_convolve(p)
                       it+=1
                   else:
                       it+=1
                continue
        # perform move
        if i == j: continue
        if d > p[i]: d = 0.5*float(p[i])
        if d <= 1e-15: continue
        old_i=float(p[i]); old_j=float(p[j])
        p[i]-=d; p[j]+=d
        q=p
        sc=float(scorer(p))
        if np.isfinite(sc) and (sc < cur - 1e-12 or rng.random() < math.exp(-(sc-cur)/max(T,1e-12))):
            cur=sc
            if sc < best_s - 1e-13:
                best_s=sc; best=p.copy()
            conv=auto_convolve(p)
        else:
            p[i]=old_i; p[j]=old_j
        it+=1
        # periodic ops every 80 iterations if enough time
        if it % 80 == 0:
            for cand in (symmetrize_p(p), smoothed_p(p, max(1,int(n*0.01))), smoothed_p(p, max(1,int(n*0.03)))):
                if cand is None: continue
                sc=float(scorer(cand))
                if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < cur - 1e-12:
                    p=cand.copy(); cur=sc
                    if sc < best_s: best_s=sc; best=p.copy()
                    conv=auto_convolve(p)
                    break
    return best, best_s
```
Need if `rng.choice` p includes NaN? We ensure finite. If p array has zeros? src_w has 1e-15 so positive._OK.
`math.exp` with negative large underflows to 0, okay. If cur is inf? We fallback fast for cur. If sc inf, `sc < cur` false, exp inf? Check `np.isfinite(sc)` before accept; if not, reject. Good.
For periodic smoothing, if accepted, no conv refresh? we do.

Potential bug: For `r<0.65` but `sens` fallback no move, `i,j` may be unbound? We set move_done True only if maxsens >...; else not. Good.
For random branch, if i==j continue leaves i/j small values? next loop.

`evolution_search` implementation:
```
def evolution_search(p0,budget,rng,scorer,pop_size=6):
   p0=normalize_positive(p0)
   if p0 is None: return None, inf
   pop=[]
   starts=[p0, symmetrize_p(p0), smoothed_p(p0, max(1,int(p0.size*0.02))), mutate_sequence(p0,0.06,rng,2), mix_uniform(p0,0.1), mutate_sequence(p0,0.15,rng,4)]
   rid=0
   for cand in starts:
      if cand is None: continue
      sc=float(scorer(cand))
      if not np.isfinite(sc): sc=1e100
      pop.append((cand, sc, rid))
      rid+=1
   if not pop: return p0, fast_score_normalized(p0)
   best=pop[0][0].copy(); best_s=pop[0][1]
   for p,sc,rid in pop:
      if sc < best_s:
          best_s=sc; best=p.copy()
   start=time.monotonic()
   mu=min(3,pop_size)
   while time.monotonic()-start < budget:
      frac=min(1.0,(time.monotonic()-start)/budget)
      sigma=0.08*(1.0-frac)+0.004
      offspring=[]
      # sort current pop to list with rids for sorting
      sorted_pop=[]
      for idx,(p,sc,rid_) in enumerate(pop):
          sorted_pop.append((sc, rid_, p))
      sorted_pop.sort()
      # build current list for selection: finite first? keep all
      for i in range(pop_size):
          if len(sorted_pop) == 0: break
          # parent selection
          top_count=min(mu, len(sorted_pop))
          if float(rng.random()) < 0.15:
              par=sorted_pop[int(rng.integers(len(sorted_pop)))]
          else:
              top=sorted_pop[:top_count]
              weights=np.arange(1, len(top)+1, dtype=float)
              weights /= weights.sum()
              idx=int(rng.choice(len(top), p=weights))
              par=top[idx]
          q=mutate_sequence(par[2], sigma, rng, moves=3)
          if q is None: q=par[2].copy()
          scq=float(scorer(q))
          if not np.isfinite(scq): scq=1e100
          offspring.append((q, scq, rid_)) # rid_ reuse? Need unique id for offspring. We'll assign unique later.
      # update best
      for q,scq,rid_ in offspring:
          if scq < best_s:
              best_s=scq; best=q.copy()
      # combine and select top pop_size
      combined=[]
      u=rdid? Need unique per entry. We can use a counter local variable; no nesting.
```
We need unique int. Use `uid=0`; for current `uid +=1`; but if previous rids not unique? Assign newly.
```

      for p,sc,rid_ in pop:
          if np.isfinite(sc):
             combined.append((sc, uid, p)); uid+=1
      for q,scq,rid_ in offspring:
          if np.isfinite(scq):
             combined.append((scq, uid, q)); uid+=1
      if not combined:
          combined=[(1e100,0,p0.copy())]
      combined.sort()
      pop=[]
      uid=0
      for sc,_,p in combined[:pop_size]:
          pop.append((p,sc,uid)); uid+=1
   return best,best_s
```
In offspring creation, `rid_` from par irrelevant. For uid unique in combined.

`generate_seed_candidates`:
```
def generate_seed_candidates(parent,lengths,rng):
   cands=[]
   for n in lengths:
      n=int(n)
      if n<4: continue
      if parent is not None:
         pi=interp_to_length(parent,n)
         if pi is not None: add_unique_sequence(cands, pi)
      add_unique_sequence(cands, np.ones(n)/n)
      x=np.arange(n,dtype=float)
      g=np.exp(-0.5*((x-0.5*n)/(0.2*n+1e-9))**2)
      add_unique_sequence(cands,g)
      # noise uniform log
      base=np.ones(n)/n
      noise=base*np.exp(rng.normal(0.0,0.6,n))
      add_unique_sequence(cands,noises)
      # random dirichlet
      add_unique_sequence(cands,rng.dirichlet(np.ones(n)*0.7))
      # bimodal if n>=8
      if n>=8:
         w=max(1,n//6)
         bi=np.zeros(n); bi[:w]=1.0; bi[-w:]=1.0
         add_unique_sequence(cands,bi)
      # chirp
      if n>=16:
         ch=0.5*(1.0+np.cos(2.0*np.pi*x*x/(2.0*n)))
         add_unique_sequence(cands,ch)
   return cands
```
Need ensure `noise` variable. Use `np.exp`, may overflow? std0.6 okay. Good.

`optimize_candidate`:

def optimize_candidate(p0,budget,rng,close_fast,oracle_available):
   p=normalize_positive(p0)
   if p is None: return None,float('inf')
   n=p.size

   if not np.isfinite(best_sc): best_sc=fast_score_generic(p)

   # Stage 1 local quick

           sc1= safe_eval
       else:
           sc1= fast_score_generic
       p1,b1s=local_refine(p,b1,rng,sc1)
       if p1 is not None:
          p=p1
          if oracle_available:
             sc=p safe_eval ... update
          else: ...
   # Stage 2 fast LB if close

       if p2 is not None:

       if oracle_available and (not close_fast) and n <= 1200:

           final_sc=safe_eval
       else:

       b5=rem*0.85
       p5,b5s=local_refine(p,b5,rng,final_sc)

          p=p5; eval update
   return best,best_sc

Need after each `p=p2` update `best` via actual. Since `p2` might not be best actual; but we should also consider keeping previous p if actual worse. We can evaluate and if better update best; for `p` for further stages, if p2 actual worse but fast better, using it might be okay for fast stage but could harm actual later. If not close, p2 not used. If close, actual ~fast so okay. We'll set `p=p2` only if actual finite and <= current actual + maybe small? Simpler set p=p2; best tracked anyway. If p2 actual much worse but fast better due hidden? If close false we skip. If close true, not much.
But `best_seq` for further stages should ideally be actual best if use actual local. If we set p to p2 worse actual, local might start from bad. We should maintain `work_p` as actual best? For fast close, work_p can be fast best. For not close, keep actual best. Let's manage:
- `best_seq`, `best_sc` actual; `work` sequence for stages based on objective used.
At start `work=p`.
After a stage that uses fast and is close, update work to new p if fast score exists, and update best actual if improves. If stage not close, only update work if new actual <= best_sc (so not move away).
Simpler: After any stage, evaluate new actual. If it is better than current actual best, set both work and best to new. If equal or worse but close_fast true and stage is fast, set work to new (for further fast optimization) but keep best actual. If not close, don't set work to worse. Need implement manually.
We can maintain `work` and `work_fast` maybe.

`propose_candidate` detailed:
```
def propose_candidate(seed=42,budget_s=2400):
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    t0=time.monotonic()
    budget=float(budget_s) if finite else 2400
    deadline=t0 + max(0.0,budget - 60.0)
    parent=get_parent_sequence()
    # lengths: include parent length and common
    lengths=[]
    if parent is not None:
       lparent=parent.size
       if lparent not in lengths: lengths.append(lparent)
    for n in [64,128,192,256,384,512,768,1024,1536,2048]:
       if n not in lengths: lengths.append(n)
    # sort lengths to process? just list
    candidates=generate_seed_candidates(parent,lengths,rng)
    # quick score all
    scored=[]
    for i,cand in enumerate(candidates):
       fs=fast_score_generic(cand)
       if np.isfinite(fs): scored.append((fs,i,cand))
    scored.sort()
    selected=[]
    if parent is not None: add_unique_sequence(selected,parent)
    # parent interpolation perhaps already top but add unique
    # uniform of parent length or 256
    ulen=parent.size if parent is not None else 256
    add_unique_sequence(selected, np.ones(ulen)/ulen)
    # add top fast up to 12 total
    for fs,i,cand in scored:
       add_unique_sequence(selected,cand)
       if len(selected)>=12: break
    # add a couple perturbations of parent length
    if parent is not None:
       for alpha in (0.05,0.15):
           q=mutate_sequence(parent, alpha if alpha<0.5 else 0.05, rng, moves=4) # mutate expects sigma; alpha maybe 0.05
           add_unique_sequence(selected,q)
    # evaluate actual for selected up to 12
    evaluated=[]
    oracle_ok=_ORACLE_AVAILABLE
    for i,cand in enumerate(selected[:12]):
       ac = safe_eval(cand) if oracle_ok else float('inf')
       fs = fast_score_generic(cand)
       evaluated.append((ac, i, cand, fs))
    # Determine close_fast
    close_fast=True if oracle_ok else True
    if oracle_ok:
       for ac,i,cand,fs in evaluated:
           if np.isfinite(ac) and np.isfinite(fs):
               tol=1e-4*max(1.0,abs(ac),abs(fs))
               if abs(ac-fs) > tol:
                   close_fast=False
                   break
       if not close_fast:
           print("Oracle differs from fast proxy; using oracle-driven local search.")
       else:
           print("Oracle matches fast proxy.")
    # global best from evaluated finite ac, else fast
    best_seq=None; best_score=float('inf')
    if oracle_ok:
       finite_eval=[(ac,i,cand,fs) for ...? no lambda. We'll loop.
       for ac,i,cand,fs in evaluated:
          if np.isfinite(ac) and ac < best_score:
              best_score=ac; best_seq=cand.copy()
       # If none finite? maybe oracle returns inf due sum? but parent valid. Use fast best.

```
def make_probability(weights):
   w=np.asarray(weights,float).copy()
   w=np.where(np.isfinite(w), np.maximum(w,0.0), 0.0)
   s=w.sum()
   if s <=0: return None
   w/=s
   # adjust last
   drift=1.0 - float(w.sum())
   if abs(drift) > 0:
      w[-1] += drift
      if w[-1] < 0: w[-1]=0; w=w/w.sum()
   return w
```

Detailed `optimize_candidate` code:
```
def optimize_candidate(p0,budget,rng,close_fast,oracle_available,exclude_lag0):
    p=normalize_positive(p0)
    if p is None: return None, float('inf')
    work=p.copy()
    best=p.copy()
    if oracle_available:
       best_sc=safe_eval(best)
    else:
       best_sc=fast_score_generic(best, False)

Let's define `make_probability(weights)` top-level:
```
def make_probability(weights):
    w=np.asarray(weights,dtype=float).ravel().copy()
    if w.size==0: return None
    w=np.where(np.isfinite(w), np.maximum(w,0.0), 0.0)
    s=float(w.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300: return None
    w /= s
    drift = 1.0 - float(w.sum())
    if abs(drift) > 1e-12:
        w[-1] += drift
        if w[-1] < 0.0:
            w = np.where(np.isfinite(w), np.maximum(w,0.0), 0.0)
            s=float(w.sum())
            if s <=0: return None
            w/=s
        else:
            # ensure no negative
            w=np.maximum(w,0.0)
            s=float(w.sum())
            if s <=0: return None
            w/=s
    return w
```
If drift makes last >1? If all zeros except? okay. After adding drift, maybe sum exactly.

if src_prob is None: src_prob=np.full(n,1.0/n)
...
j=int(rng.choice(n, p=tgt_prob))
```
